# Audit findings ledger

Carried across `/weekend-audit` runs by the `audit-loop` skill. **Never delete a row** — close it
with a date. A finding that reappears after being closed is a much stronger signal than a fresh
one, and only this file can show that. An open row surviving 3 runs is itself a finding.

Lanes: **FIX** (mechanically safe) · **EVIDENCE** (touches a score/weight/threshold — needs a
measurement first) · **INVESTIGATE** (symptom, unproven cause) · **ACCEPT** (known, bounded).

| ID | Found | Class | Finding | Lane | Status | Immunized | Closed |

| AF-20260910-08 | 2026-09-10 | ml-model | **`dl_engine.walk_forward_validate` was not a walk-forward: it sliced a symbol-major panel by ROW POSITION, so every fold trained and tested over the SAME calendar dates.** `train_lstm` builds its validation panel by concatenating whole per-symbol arrays (`load_sequences_bounded` yields "in completion order"), discards the loader's 5th return value (the dates), and handed the array to a function that split it with `X[:train_end]` / `X[val_end:test_end]`. Measured against live `feature_store` before the fix (50 symbols, 59,702 sequences, the function's own `min_train=300`/`fold_size=2000`): fold 0 had 22.1% of test dates also in the training slice, fold 1 had 99.9%, **folds 2-27 had 100.0%**, train and test both spanning 2021-03-31..2026-09-09, with **zero symbols shared** between them. It was an expanding CROSS-SECTIONAL split -- train on ~40 stocks, test on ~3 others on the same days -- and daily equity direction is dominated by a market-wide common factor, so it leaks heavily. This is why the DL engine reported `roc_auc` 0.6459-0.6578 in `dl_model_config.json` while every other engine on this platform ceilings at 0.52-0.55 (`measurement.md`) -- the "implausibly high score" tell `ml-model-bugs.md` already records for `movement_predictor`. `purged_cv.py` has existed for exactly this since before the incident and its own docstring names the hazard; `ml_ensemble.py`, `breakout_classifier.py` and `flyer_classifier.py` all use it. `dl_engine.py` was the one model that did not. | FIX | **closed 2026-09-10.** `walk_forward_validate` now takes a REQUIRED `dates` vector and delegates fold construction to `_date_folds` -> `purged_cv.make_purged_group_time_series_split`, purged by `LABEL_HORIZON_DAYS=15` (the 15d head is fit on the same rows, so 5 would not cover the labels the folds train on). `train_lstm` threads the per-symbol dates through. Every `roc_auc` recorded before this date is inflated and is no longer allowed to act as a promotion baseline -- see AF-20260910-11. | 12 tests in `src/server/__tests__/test_dl_walkforward_date_split.py`, all watched RED first: fold ordering, zero train/test date overlap, purge >= horizon, cross-sectional completeness, plus an end-to-end check that encodes each row's date ordinal in `X` and asserts `max(train) < min(test)` through the real code path. | 2026-09-10 |
| AF-20260910-09 | 2026-09-10 | ml-model | **`train_lstm`'s validation loader omitted the feature-width pin its training loader carries, so an infer-then-train sequence in one process silently skews validation against training.** `_load` passes `n_features=N_FEATURES` with a comment explaining why; `_load_val` called `load_symbol_sequences(sym)` bare, falling through to `_resolve_input_width(None)` -> `_INFERENCE_INPUT_WIDTH`, a module global that `run_inference` sets to the ACTIVE champion's width. lstm_v3, the champion, is a pre-widening **78**-input checkpoint against today's `N_FEATURES=85`. `python_api.py` (ml-api :8000) imports `dl_engine` at module level and `backend-python/main.py` (:8002) imports it per-call (cached in `sys.modules` either way), and BOTH expose `/api/train-dl` and `/api/infer-dl` from the same process -- so infer-then-train there feeds 78-wide validation rows to an 85-wide model, and the size mismatch is swallowed by the caller's broad `except` as "validation failed (non-fatal)", leaving NaN metrics and a gate that can promote nothing. **Latent, not live:** nothing in `src/**/*.ts` calls `trainDL`/`inferDL` (grep found only the definitions in `pythonApi.ts`), and the scheduled jobs run `dl_engine.py --mode infer` and `dl_trainer.py` as separate subprocesses. | FIX | **closed 2026-09-10** -- one-line pin, `n_features=N_FEATURES`, matching the training loader. | `test_both_loaders_request_the_current_feature_width` asserts every `load_symbol_sequences` call from `train_lstm` pins the width, so a future `None` fails instead of silently inheriting a champion's shape. Watched RED. | 2026-09-10 |
| AF-20260910-10 | 2026-09-10 | data-integrity | **The unit test suite writes a hardcoded metric sentinel into production `dl_model_performance`, overwriting the day's real monitoring row.** Found by watching a new dl_engine test print `[DRIFT] persisted held-out metrics for 2026-09-10`. `train_lstm()` calls `drift_detector.write_training_metrics(metrics, model_version=f"lstm_v{version}")` on every run, and `test_dl_engine.py::test_train_lstm_calls_walk_forward_validate` drives `train_lstm(version=99)` with a hardcoded `{"directional_accuracy": 0.55, "roc_auc": 0.58}` sentinel. A developer's Postgres IS production, so it landed. Live: **10 rows with `model_version='lstm_v99'`, 2026-08-27..2026-09-10, holding exactly two distinct metric pairs -- `(0.55, 0.58)` (the sentinel) and `(None, None)`.** Worse than an extra row: the upsert key is `(model_name, eval_date, horizon_days)` and does NOT include `model_version`, so one test run REPLACES that date's genuine row, and `check_accuracy_drift` then reads a unit-test constant as the DL model's held-out accuracy baseline. Same class as the 2026-09-09 Telegram incident in `recurring-bugs.md`. | FIX | **closed 2026-09-10, code and data.** Writer guarded; the 10 fabricated rows **deleted** with user approval (`DELETE FROM dl_model_performance WHERE model_version='lstm_v99'` -> 10 rows). Verified after: 0 `lstm_v99` rows remain and the genuine `current` series is intact (42 rows, 2026-07-11..2026-09-09). The real rows these had overwritten are unrecoverable; deleting leaves a GAP on those dates rather than a fabricated reading, which is the honest state. Exact deleted set: nine rows at `(0.55, 0.58)` on 08-27, 08-29, 08-30, 09-01, 09-02, 09-03, 09-05, 09-06, 09-08, plus the 09-10 row -- that last one read `(None, None)` when first observed and `(0.5, 0.5)` by deletion time, because THIS session's own test runs rewrote it before the guard landed, which is itself the cleanest demonstration of the defect. | `write_training_metrics` is now inert under `PYTEST_CURRENT_TEST` unless `DRIFT_ALLOW_TEST_WRITES=1`, mirroring the Telegram fix's runtime guard. Two tests in `src/server/__tests__/test_drift_metrics_no_test_writes.py`: one that no DB call happens under pytest (watched RED), one that the writer still fires with the override -- so the guard discriminates rather than disabling monitoring. | 2026-09-10 |
| AF-20260910-11 | 2026-09-10 | ml-model | **The DL promotion gate would have been frozen by construction the moment validation became honest.** `_promote_lstm_version` compares a candidate's `roc_auc` against the active version's stored number + a 0.005 margin. The active v3 baseline is **0.58**, produced by the row-sliced split of AF-20260910-08; nothing validated by a purged date split can beat that, so every future candidate would reject on merit it never had a chance to show. Identical shape to the `ml_ensemble.py` label switch `ml-model-bugs.md` records (CV 0.7664 -> 0.5203: "a gate comparing 0.5203 against a 0.7664 baseline rejects every candidate of the new label, permanently, by construction rather than on merit"). | FIX | **closed 2026-09-10.** Metrics now carry a `validation_method` tag (`purged_date_walkforward_v1`); when the baseline's method differs from the candidate's, the metric bar is skipped, exactly like `model_promotion.promotion_decision`'s `label_changed` branch. | 4 tests in `test_dl_engine_promotion_gate.py`: a legacy baseline cannot block an honestly-validated candidate (watched RED); a same-method baseline still blocks a worse one; **two untagged versions are compared normally** (keyed on a CHANGE of method, never on absence of a tag, or "untagged" would silently mean "promote anything" -- the guard `ml-model-bugs.md` demands alongside any such override); and the promoted row records how it was measured. | 2026-09-10 |
| AF-20260910-06 | 2026-09-10 | data-sources | **A throttled vendor run was indistinguishable from an empty universe: `finstack_cashflow_fetcher.py` counted every Yahoo rate-limit as "no vendor coverage" and exited 0.** Surfaced from a pm2 log full of `finstack.data.fundamentals ERROR: Error fetching cash flow for <SYM>: Too Many Requests. Rate limited.` — 8+ symbols per SECOND, i.e. 6 parallel workers hammering with no backoff. finstack wraps yfinance, and Yahoo's throttle reply is the same `{"error": true}` envelope shape as a genuine "no quarterly cash flow for this ticker", so `parse_quarterly_cashflow()` flattened both to `[]`, `run()` tallied both as `empty`, and `main()` returned 0 unconditionally — a run that wrote NOTHING because the vendor refused every call recorded as a clean success. Live state: `finstack_cashflow_history` holds **59 rows / 15 symbols** for a ~2,000-symbol weekly job (since 2026-09-01), and how much of that gap is true Yahoo coverage vs. accumulated throttling is **not knowable** for the historical rows — the docstring's "Yahoo only covers a subset" claim had been absorbing it. Second occurrence of a class whose first fix (`insider_transactions_fetcher`, 2026-07-31, `None` vs `[]`) was recorded only in memory and never promoted to `.claude/rules/` — now added there. | FIX | Classify the throttle BEFORE parsing (`is_rate_limited_envelope()`, pure + tested) and raise `RateLimited` from `fetch_symbol()`; count it separately from `empty`; back off `RATE_LIMIT_BACKOFF_SEC`; **abort the run at `RATE_LIMIT_ABORT_THRESHOLD=25`** rather than burning the remaining universe and keeping the throttle warm; emit a DEGRADED line to **stderr** (runPython only inspects stderr) and **exit 1** when throttled. `parse_quarterly_cashflow()` left pure with its contract unchanged. Docstring corrected so it no longer teaches the wrong conclusion. | new `test_rate_limit_vs_no_coverage.py` 7/7, negative-controlled (2 fail against a reverted classifier; the "genuine no-coverage is NOT throttling" discrimination control stays green, so the fix cannot have inverted the bug); existing 12 fetcher tests still pass; full pytest **2565 passed / 0 failed** | 2026-09-10 |
| AF-20260910-07 | 2026-09-10 | job-scheduling-reliability | **`mf_holdings_fetcher.py` timed out at its 20-min budget and, because its only write ran AFTER the loop, the entire run was discarded.** `[PY] mf_holdings_fetcher.py ... Timed out after 1200000ms`. Arithmetic: ~1,400 resolvable symbols × `RATE_LIMIT_SEC = 0.3` is **~7 min of pure sleep** before a single HTTP response is counted, plus 1,400 sequential requests — a budget with no headroom, which tipped over today. It is **not** permanently broken (`stock_mf_holdings` holds 8,404 rows / 1,403 symbols; 09-08 succeeded), but each overrun loses everything: the accumulate-then-`upsert_holdings()`-once-at-the-end shape is this repo's documented "a step at the END of a script that routinely gets killed by its timeout never runs" class. Root cause of the wasted work is the same as AF-20260909-15: **quarterly** SEBI shareholding filings were being fully recrawled **weekly**. | FIX | Matched fetch cadence to data cadence — added `STALENESS_DAYS = 80` + `recently_fetched()` so symbols written within a quarter are skipped (steady-state runs become a near-no-op, so the timeout cannot recur), with `--no-skip` to force a full refetch. Made writes **incremental** (`FLUSH_EVERY = 100`) so a kill can no longer discard the run. `recently_fetched()` degrades to an EMPTY set on any error — toward MORE work, never toward silently skipping the universe. | negative-controlled test that an unreadable skip-list fetches everything rather than nothing; `py_compile` clean; full pytest **2565 passed / 0 failed** | 2026-09-10 |
| AF-20260910-05 | 2026-09-10 | deploy-reliability | **The whole platform stops whenever the host sleeps or reboots, nothing restarts it, and NOTHING detects it — measured at ~40h of silent downtime across 14 days.** Surfaced from two pm2 log lines the user pasted (`exit_policy.py ... exit code 1073807364`, `ml_ensemble.py ... exit code 3221225794`) that looked like Python crashes and are not: `0x40010004` = DBG_TERMINATE_PROCESS and `0xC0000142` = STATUS_DLL_INIT_FAILED, i.e. Windows tearing down the process tree. Confirmed via System event log 1074 — `TrustedInstaller.exe ... Operating System: Upgrade (Planned)` restarted the box three times at 02:04/02:05/02:06 IST; `LastBootUpTime` = 02:06:44. The in-flight `ml-weekly-retrain` make-up died with it. **Services then stayed down 4.4h** (last job 2026-09-09T20:30Z, next 2026-09-10T00:56Z) because there is **no pm2 Windows service and no scheduled task** (`Get-Service`/`Get-ScheduledTask` both empty; `pm2 startup` does not support Windows) — pm2 only came back when a human started it. This is not a one-off: measured over 14d/15,927 intervals, inter-job gaps run p50 0.23min / p95 5.2min / p99 14.5min, yet **nine gaps exceeded 2h** (8.6h, 7.7h, 6.3h, 4.4h, 3.7h, 3.1h, 2.5h, 2.1h, 2.1h). Every existing heartbeat/fail-rate check read "healthy" throughout, because a job that never ran writes no failure row. | FIX | **(a)** `pythonRunner.ts` now decodes abnormal exit codes (`describeExitCode`/`isHostTeardownExit`) so an OS-killed child reports `HOST/OS TERMINATION: DBG_TERMINATE_PROCESS (0x40010004) — ...` instead of a bare magic number; an OS-killed process writes nothing to either stream, which is why the old fallback produced an unactionable number. Ordinary exit 1 messages are byte-identical (negative-controlled). **(b)** new repo-doctor check `platform-outage-gaps` (WARN on any >90min window with zero job runs in 7d — ~6x the measured p99, backtested to catch all nine real outages and none of the healthy intervals). **(c)** `scripts/install-pm2-autostart.ps1` registers a Scheduled Task running `pm2 resurrect` at logon/wake. **(a)+(b) landed; (c) is written but NOT yet run — it changes machine state and needs the user to execute it.** **UPDATE 2026-09-10 (later): (a)/(b) are now deployed** (see AF-20260910-04). **(c) `scripts/install-pm2-autostart.ps1` had never been executed and did not work.** Running it for the first time surfaced two bugs the "written but deliberately NOT executed" decision had hidden: (1) a **U+2014 em-dash on line 71** in a file with no BOM -- Windows PowerShell 5.1 reads a BOM-less file as ANSI, so the multibyte char broke string parsing and the script died at PARSE time with a misleading "missing terminator" pointing at line 81; (2) `[string]$RepoDir = (Split-Path -Parent $PSScriptRoot)` as a **parameter default** -- under `powershell.exe -File`, `$PSScriptRoot` is not yet populated during parameter binding, so it threw "Cannot bind argument to parameter 'Path' because it is an empty string". Both fixed (ASCII hyphen; resolve in the body with a `$MyInvocation.MyCommand.Path` fallback) and the script now runs correctly end to end -- it resolves the repo dir, finds pm2, and builds the right command. **It still fails at the last step: `Register-ScheduledTask : Access is denied` (HRESULT 0x80070005) -- registration needs an ELEVATED shell, which a non-interactive session cannot obtain.** Verified after: `Get-ScheduledTask -TaskName 'bharat-pm2-resurrect'` -> NOT REGISTERED. Repo-wide scan found no other `.ps1` carrying a non-ASCII character, so bug (1) is a one-off, not a class. **Remaining user action: run `powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File scripts/install-pm2-autostart.ps1` from an Administrator PowerShell.** Until then the platform still does not come back on its own after a reboot or resume, which is a live risk to tonight's queued runs (AF-20260910-12/-13). | vitest `pythonRunnerExitCodes.test.ts` 3/3, negative-controlled (2 of 3 fail against the reverted decoder, the "ordinary path unchanged" control stays green); repo-doctor `platform-outage-gaps` verified firing on the 5 real windows in the live 7d data; `tsc --noEmit` clean; full unit suite 1256 passing | (a)(b) 2026-09-10; **(c) needs an elevated shell** |

Applied via `npm run migrate:up` in one run, no failures. `npm run schema:regen` + `schema:drift` clean (224=224). **Two real correctness bugs found and fixed while verifying, both live-reproduced before and after**: (1) psycopg2 casts a native DATE column to a Python `datetime.date` object by default — every Python reader would have silently started receiving a different type than before (breaking string ops / `json.dumps`); fixed by registering a global type caster in `db_compat.py` mirroring `pgClient.ts`'s existing `types.setTypeParser(DATE, v => v)`, live-verified to return `'2026-09-03'` as `str` both for a newly-converted table and for `technical_signals.date` (native DATE since 2026-08-25, which had NEVER had this caster — a latent bug this fix also retroactively closed). (2) **The migration's "no application code changes" assumption was false and the blast radius was larger than the migration itself** — 20+ call sites across `ml_ensemble.py` (~35 occurrences across 4 near-duplicate query blocks), `as_of.py` (`as_of_join_sql()`'s own `base_date_is_text` cast-direction formula), `exit_policy.py`, `ml_calibration.py`, `ml_signal_scorer.py`, `outcome_resolver.py`, `performance_tracker.py`, `backfill_technical_features.py` (comment only), `ml.router.ts`, `syncProprietaryScores.ts`, `technicalSignalsService.ts` had a defensive `::text` cast written for the *old* TEXT type that now forces a `date = text`/`date >= text` mismatch against the *new* DATE type — found via the full test suite (not a targeted search): 5 pytest failures (`test_fundamentals_pit.py` x3, `test_exit_policy.py` x2) plus 1 vitest failure (a golden-SQL-string assertion in `syncTrendlyneScores.test.ts` that had to be updated, not just the source) surfaced the pattern, then a full repo grep for the same shape found and fixed the rest before re-confirming. **Final state, live-verified twice**: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; `npx vitest run` 1125/1125 passed (0 failed) twice consecutively; `pytest` (full suite minus 7 files blocked by an unrelated pre-existing torch DLL load issue on this Windows venv, confirmed pre-existing via `git stash` on `db_compat.py`) 2218/2218 passed (0 failed) twice consecutively. Live-queried both shared feature-query functions (`full_feature_train_sql('so','signal_date')` and `full_feature_score_sql()`) end-to-end against real production data post-fix, both returned real rows with correctly-typed `signal_date`/`date` fields. `bharat-server`, `ml-api`, `alphaquant-api`, `chatbot`, `engine-worker` all restarted and confirmed `online`. **ID corrected 2026-09-02**: this row and the `AF-20260831-01` monitoring finding below (line ~597) were both filed under `AF-20260831-01` — a genuine ID collision in this ledger, found during the 2026-09-02 consolidation pass. | AF-20260818-45 | 2026-08-18 | tooling | **AF-20260816-18's "fixed + verified" streaming claim for `integrity_sweep.py` does not reproduce for a full-repo run** — 0 bytes of output after 20+ minutes. Measured: `technical_signals` alone (303 cols) took **8.07s** for its 303 sequential round trips. Over ~213 tables the accumulated round-trip count, not buffering, is why nothing printed for 20+ minutes — most early tables (alphabetically) have zero findings, so nothing streams, but every one of them still pays its full per-column cost before moving on. Rewrote to ONE query per table via conditional aggregation (`COUNT(col)`/`COUNT(DISTINCT col)` for every column in a single `SELECT`), with a per-column fallback preserved for a table where the batched query itself errors (an exotic/unorderable type must not silently drop every other column's check). `technical_signals`: 8.07s → 3.2s for that table; **full-repo run now completes in 9m05s** (previously: never completed, abandoned at 20+ min both times it was tried). Verified byte-identical output before/after on 6 tables checked in the same instant (`technical_signals`, `unified_recommendations`, `feature_store`, `quant_scores`, `stock_scores`, `marketsmojo_financials_history`) — later drift in `technical_signals`' own dead-column count (58→66 across ~30 min) is live production data changing under a concurrently-running enrichment job, not a regression, confirmed by re-running the scoped check at that later instant and getting 66 both times. 9 minutes for 213 tables is a real, large improvement, not "fast" — flagged honestly, not oversold | **rung 4** — new `test_integrity_sweep.py`, 5 tests pinning `_classify()` (the decision logic both the batched and fallback paths now share) against every outcome: dead, frozen-numeric, not-frozen-text, populated-and-varying, partial-null. Extracting `_classify()` out of the original inline loop is itself the change that makes both paths guaranteed to agree | 2026-08-18 |

Fixed by replacing the plain `LEFT JOIN` with `LEFT JOIN LATERAL (... ORDER BY computed_at DESC LIMIT 1) cs ON true` — same sargable half-open range (still chunk-exclusion-friendly, still never wraps `computed_at` in `date()`), now takes only the latest row per symbol inside it | **rung 4** — new `technicalConfluenceSignalsNoDuplicates.test.ts`, seeds one `technical_signals` row + 2 same-day `confluence_signals` rows (different `computed_at`/score) against the real router via `createCallerFactory`, asserts exactly 1 output row carrying the LATEST score. Negative-controlled: fails `expected 2 to be 1` against the pre-fix source (`git stash`), passes restored | 2026-08-18 |

Reproduced live via `liveStockData.live.test.ts`: Yahoo's batch endpoint missed 412/2550 symbols in one pass; `fetchAllLiveStocks()`'s per-symbol fallback only runs when `missed.length <= MAX_INDIVIDUAL_FALLBACKS` (env-tunable, default **250**) — at 412 missed, the code takes the `else` branch and skips fallback for **all** 412, RELIANCE included, logging only a `console.warn`. Confirmed RELIANCE actually has no 2026-09-03 row in production `stock_ohlcv` afterward. **Not itself proof of a standing production outage** — the real scheduled `stock-refresh` job runs once/day at market close (10:30 UTC/16:00 IST, cron `30 10 * * 1-5`, `queues.ts:1695`; contradicts that same file's own top-of-file comment claiming "every 5 min" — that comment is stale, the local comment at the schedule site is correct and deliberate: "Daily sync after market close... ensures OHLCV data is persisted for backtesting") and RELIANCE has an unbroken row for every recent session (09-01, 08-31, 08-28...), so this was very likely a one-off Yahoo batch hiccup coinciding with this test run, not an ongoing daily loss. **The real gap is the monitoring, not (necessarily) today's fetch**: `ohlcv-freshness-coverage` (`dataQualityChecks.ts:670`) only checks `COUNT(DISTINCT symbol) >= 300` over a **rolling 10-day window** — a single day where 16% of the ~2,550-symbol universe (or specific NIFTY constituents) silently drops out would not trip it; 9 other good days paper over one bad one. | AF-20260903-05 | 2026-09-03 | measurement | **A "7-day" and a "30-day" lookback window in `ml_ensemble.py`'s feature queries were silently one day short, for as long as the columns involved were TEXT.** Found while reviewing AF-20260831-04's own diff, not by a test. The pre-migration SQL read `AND mp2.date >= (<anchor>::date - interval '7 days')::text` — Postgres renders `DATE - interval` as a **timestamp**, so `::text` produced `'2026-08-27 00:00:00'`, and the left side (`mc_pricefeed_daily.date`, then TEXT) held `'2026-08-27'`. That comparison is lexicographic, and `'2026-08-27' >= '2026-08-27 00:00:00'` is **FALSE** (the shorter string sorts first), so the boundary day was excluded: the 7-day `mc_pricefeed_daily` fallback window was effectively 6 days, and the 30-day `gdelt_sentiment` news-sentiment average was effectively 29. Verified empirically against live Postgres, both directions: old form → boundary excluded, new form → boundary included. | FIX | **closed** 2026-09-03 — fixed incidentally-but-really by AF-20260831-04's `::text` removal: the comparisons are now `mp2.date >= (<anchor> - interval '7 days')` with both sides native DATE/timestamp, so the boundary day is correctly included. Affects `full_feature_train_sql()` (used by `cs_ranker.py`, `exit_policy.py`, `online_learner.py`), `full_feature_score_sql()`, and `load_training_data()`'s own inline copies. **Stated honestly per `measurement.md`: this is a correctness fix with an UNMEASURED forward effect, not a claimed improvement** — same posture as the 2026-08-22 `_blend` normalization fix ("ship it because the code now means what it says, not because it improved anything"). The affected inputs are a same-day-fallback price row and a 30-day news-tone average, so the practical delta is confined to boundary days; the next ensemble retrain's feature matrix will differ very slightly from prior ones at those boundaries, which is expected and should not be read as drift. `ml_ensemble.py` is not in `verify-gate.mjs`'s backtest-evidence list and no weight, threshold or blend was touched. | — (a static check for "`::text` applied to a date-interval expression" would be narrow and this class is now covered by the `recurring-bugs.md` inverted-recurrence entry AF-20260831-04 added) | 2026-09-03 |

| AF-20260904-01 | 2026-09-04 | data-coverage | **`mc_chart_patterns_fetcher.py`'s bulk-API rewrite collapsed `mc_pattern_signals` coverage from 2,338 stocks/day to 68 — a 97% loss — and no monitor can see it.** Found reviewing a concurrent session's uncommitted working-tree change (not yet committed at time of writing). The rewrite replaces ~2,328 per-stock calls to `/technicalpicks/chart-patterns?sc_id=<mcsymbol>` with ONE market-wide call to the same endpoint without `sc_id`, which is a genuine ~14min-to-0.6s win on fetch time but **is not the same work**: the bulk endpoint returns only the currently-active patterns market-wide (live-measured 2026-09-04: **82 patterns, 75 of which map to a symbol**, 7 silently dropped for having neither `sc_id` — absent from the bulk response shape entirely — nor a parseable `meta_data.price_key`). The old serial path called `compute_and_upsert_signals(sym, today, [], con)` for every stock in the universe, writing an explicit `bull_count=0, bear_count=0, net_score=0` row for the ~2,260 with no active pattern. The bulk path only visits stocks present in the response, so a stock whose pattern expired is now **absent** rather than scored zero, and `backfill_technical_signals` is never called for it — its `technical_signals` pattern columns keep the prior run's values indefinitely. Live-verified against production: `SELECT date, count(DISTINCT symbol) FROM mc_pattern_signals GROUP BY date` reads **2,337-2,338 every trading day from 2026-08-19 through 2026-09-02, then 68 on 2026-09-04** — the collapse lands exactly on the change. This is `recurring-bugs.md`'s "any table written as today's full recomputation needs a purge of rows the run did not produce" class, arrived at from the other direction (rows aren't stale, they're simply never written), compounded by the "a table-freshness check cannot see whether the FEATURE landed" class: `mc-chart-patterns-freshness` PASSES, because rows for today do exist — just 68 of them. | FIX | **closed, ACCEPTED** 2026-09-04 — user reviewed the measured evidence (2,338 symbols/day through 2026-09-02, then 68 on 2026-09-04) and explicitly accepted the trade: "am ok with it". The ~14min-to-0.6s fetch win is kept and the reduced per-day symbol coverage is an accepted consequence, not an outstanding defect. **What this row is still worth to a future reader**: `mc_pattern_signals` no longer carries an explicit zero row for a stock whose patterns expired, so any consumer joining on (symbol, date) sees the symbol as absent rather than as scored-zero, and the most recent value for such a symbol is whatever it last had — do NOT read a stale non-zero as current. `mc-chart-patterns-freshness` passes throughout by construction (rows for today exist, there are just ~68), so freshness monitoring cannot detect a further decline; the check that would is distinct-symbols-per-date as a share of the mcsymbol-bearing universe. Verified still running 2026-09-04 post-acceptance: rc=0, mc_pattern_signals 100,054 -> 100,057 rows, latest date 2026-09-04. | — (an accepted trade-off needs no immunization; the coverage-check idea is recorded above for whoever revisits it) | 2026-09-04 |

### Triage of AF-20260823-73's 6 non-ranker hits (2026-08-23)

Each site's source table was checked for weekend/holiday rows, and each empty-read traced to its
consumer. **The distinction that matters is not "does the read return empty" but "does the caller
DEGRADE or NO-OP when it does."** Two are real, three are benign, one is a true positive whose
source table saves it.

| ID | Site | Source table trading-day-only? | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| AF-20260823-74 | `scoring_engine.py:688` (`days=1`) | **yes** — `technical_signals` 25 weekend rows / 32,894 in 21d | **REAL, and the worst of the six** |
| AF-20260823-75 | `scoring_engine.py:761` (`days=3`) | **yes** | **REAL on a long weekend only** |
| AF-20260823-76 | `screener_sector_rotation.py:52` (`days=2`) | **yes** | **REAL but self-limiting** |

| ID | Found | Class | Finding | Lane | Status | Immunized | Closed |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| AF-20260823-74 | 2026-08-23 | temporal | **`scoring_engine.py:688`'s `win_prob_map` cutoff is `date.today() - 1` over `technical_signals`.** On any Monday the map comes back **empty**, and `win_prob_map.get(symbol)` then returns `None` for every symbol — which `ml_alignment_points()` converts to its `8  # neutral if no ML signal` fallback. **Measured live: the real mean is 17.71/20** (2,196 symbols on the latest date), so Factor 3 silently drops **~9.7 of 20 points on every symbol, uniformly, every Monday.** A uniform shift cannot change any *ranking*, which is exactly why it is invisible — but `stock_scores` feeds `unified_recommendations` and the platform's absolute thresholds, the same mechanism as `recurring-bugs.md`'s factor-crowding incident (a uniform ×0.9 that moved the whole population against fixed cutoffs). Same file also uses the correct pattern elsewhere, so this is an inconsistency, not a house style. | **EVIDENCE** | **CLOSED same day — see "AF-20260823-70/74/75/76 CLOSED 2026-08-23" below.** 0 → 2,196 symbols, before/after on a live production connection. Row reconciled 2026-08-29 (previously contradicted the closure block below for 6 days). | rung 1 — `check_short_calendar_lookback` + rung 4 `test_trading_day_cutoffs.py` | 2026-08-23 |
| AF-20260823-75 | 2026-08-23 | temporal | `scoring_engine.py:761`'s `sym_signal_types` cutoff is `days=3` over `technical_signals` — survives a normal Fri→Mon gap (exactly 3) but returns empty after any **long weekend / holiday Monday** (4+ calendar days). Consumer is the `win_prob_map and sym_signal_types` prior-blend at :784, which is skipped entirely when either is empty — so the blend silently does not run rather than running wrong. | EVIDENCE | **CLOSED same day — see closure block below.** 566 → 722 symbols before/after. Row reconciled 2026-08-29. | rung 1 + rung 4 | 2026-08-23 |
| AF-20260823-76 | 2026-08-23 | temporal | `screener_sector_rotation.py:52`'s primary read is `days=2` over `technical_signals` → empty on Monday. **Self-limiting**: the function's own docstring documents a `screener_appearances` fallback, and that table *does* write on weekends, so the script degrades to its secondary path rather than producing nothing. Output table `screener_sector_rotation` is fresh (max 2026-08-22, 533 rows), consistent with the fallback working. | ACCEPT | **CLOSED same day — see closure block below.** Fixed anyway (cheap, not gated): primary path now fires on Mondays instead of always falling back to the secondary path. Row reconciled 2026-08-29. | rung 1 + rung 4 | 2026-08-23 |

**Three benign, with reasons — recorded so they are not re-triaged next run:**

- **`ml_ensemble.py:3074`** — `cutoff_date` is **not a lookback filter**. It is passed to
  `_apply_plain_expiry_gate`/`_apply_regime_expiry_gate` and used as `if str(r['signal_date']) <
  cutoff_date: expire`, i.e. an age threshold for expiring `recommendation_log` rows whose
  `win_probability` is NULL. An empty trading day just means marginally fewer rows expire that
  run, self-correcting the next day. **Checker true-positive on shape, false-positive on impact** —
  the check flags `date.today() - timedelta(days<=4)` regardless of use, which is the right
  trade (shape is cheap and reliable to detect; intent is not).
- **`screener_sector_rotation.py:23`** — `YESTERDAY` reads the script's **own output table**
  (`load_previous_sector_scores`) for a momentum delta, not a trading-day source. Missing
  yesterday's row yields `{}` → momentum_change of 0, which is correct behaviour for "no prior
  snapshot".
- **`screener_signal_generator.py:84`** — source is `screener_appearances`, which is **not**
  trading-day-only: ~19-29k rows land on every weekend day (13,080 weekend rows in the last 21d).
  The 2-day window always contains data.


### AF-20260823-70/74/75/76 CLOSED 2026-08-23 — all 9 real sites fixed, measured before/after on a live Sunday

The EVIDENCE gate was satisfiable without waiting for a Monday: **2026-08-23 was a Sunday, so the
bug was live at the moment of the fix.** Both engines were exercised against production before and
after, on the same connection, minutes apart — not simulated, not reasoned about.

**`unified_ranker.py` — all 6 getters, symbols returned:**

| engine (cutoff) | BEFORE | AFTER |
|---|---|---|
| `dl` (days=1) | **0** | **2,424** |
| `confluence` (days=1) | 3,748 | 4,613 |
| `screener_momentum` (days=2) | 1,980 | 2,039 |
| `ml` (days=3) | 2,200 | 2,201 |
| `cs` (days=3) | 2,200 | 2,201 |
| `technical` (days=3) | 2,200 | 2,201 |

**`scoring_engine.py` — distinct symbols matched:**

| read | BEFORE cutoff | BEFORE | AFTER cutoff | AFTER |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `win_prob_map` (days=1) | 2026-08-22 | **0** | 2026-08-21 | **2,196** |
| `sym_signal_types` (days=3) | 2026-08-20 | 566 | 2026-08-19 | 722 |

`win_prob_map` at 0 is Factor 3 sitting on its `8  # neutral` fallback for every symbol against a
measured real mean of 17.71/20. Restored to full coverage.

All 9 now use `as_of.trading_days_back(n, conn)[-1]`, whose `[-1]` is the oldest of the n real
sessions — so `>= that` admits exactly n trading days regardless of weekends or holidays.

**Live re-verification:** `e2e_lifecycle_check.py --n 10` now **PASSes on a Sunday** (exit 0, 28
stages), which it could not do before the fix — `engine:dl` was FAILing. That is the end-to-end
proof, not just a green suite.

Negative-controlled (3 tests fail against the bug). **Second-order regression caught and fixed in the same pass**: NULLing the columns dropped them under `densify_feature_matrix.py`'s `SPARSE_COVERAGE_THRESHOLD=0.50`, making them forward-fill candidates for the first time — which would have carried an F&O name's stale wall across 120 days, strictly worse than the zeros removed. A genuine zero net FII flow is meaningful, but a *default* zero fabricated by omission is not an observation — NULL is correct for all four regardless of flow semantics. | AUTO-FIX | **CLOSED** — migration `20260823235900` drops the four DEFAULTs and repairs existing all-four-zero rows to NULL (reversible Down restores both); `db/schema.postgres.sql` aligned; both Python INSERTs now name the four columns with explicit NULLs so the fabrication-by-omission channel is closed even if a DEFAULT ever returns; all four added to `densify_feature_matrix.NEVER_FILL` (they sit far under SPARSE_COVERAGE_THRESHOLD post-repair and would otherwise become forward-fill candidates — the exact second-order trap AF-77's walls fell into). **Sweep query that found AF-78** (re-run it after any new `DEFAULT 0` column lands):

```sql
SELECT column_name FROM information_schema.columns
WHERE table_name='technical_signals' AND table_schema=current_schema()
  AND column_default IN ('0','0.0','(0)::double precision','(0)::real')
  AND data_type IN ('real','double precision','numeric','integer','bigint','smallint');
```

Then per column: `count(*) FILTER (WHERE col=0)` vs `count(DISTINCT col)`. A high zero-share with
`count(DISTINCT) > 2` on a **continuous** quantity is the signature; a 2-valued column is a flag
and almost always fine.

**AF-78 fix landed 2026-08-23**: schema defaults dropped (`db/schema.postgres.sql`) and both
grid/backfill INSERTs in `backfill_technical_features.py` now supply explicit NULLs for
`fii_10d_net` / `dii_3d_net` / `sector_ret_5d` / `sector_ret_21d` instead of omitting them.
Residual risk moved upstream: the FII/DII **fetcher** still populates very few symbols
(live 08-23: distinct≈2/day) — that is now a data-coverage problem, not a schema problem.

| AF-20260827-19 | 2026-08-27 | data-coverage | **`trendlyne-midweek` (feeds `trendlyne_adv_tech_daily`/`trendlyne_price_analysis` tables and this session's own AF-05-adjacent Trendlyne `_tl` `technical_signals` columns) has an 83.3% historical failure rate and has not succeeded once in 8+ days** (last success 2026-08-19, `job_heartbeat`: 54 runs, 45 failed). Traced to the live error log, not guessed: requests start with genuine `200`s, then degrade into persistent `HTTP 405 (x-amzn-waf-action=captcha)`. Both underlying fetchers (`trendlyne_adv_tech_fetcher.py`, `trendlyne_price_analysis_fetcher.py`) already call `cap_to_run_budget(..., limit=TRENDLYNE_RUN_REQUEST_BUDGET=110)` internally — the exact mechanism this repo already built and documented (memory: `trendlyne_waf_request_allowance_2026_08_17`) specifically to stay under the WAF's measured ~131-150-request-per-session ceiling. The cap is firing on every run and the run is still hitting captcha well before completion. **Root cause found on closer trace, not just narrowed**: `retry_get()` applied its blanket 3x exponential-backoff retry to a WAF captcha response exactly the same as a transient error — so every WAF-blocked symbol paid up to 3 requests before `FetchTracker`'s `abort_after_consecutive_fails=20` circuit breaker even counted one failure, meaning the breaker could burn up to 60 of the 110-request budget on responses that were never going to succeed (a captcha does not self-clear on retry). This compounds the request-budget math the fetcher already tries to respect, independent of whether the WAF's real ceiling moved. | FIX | **fixed, committed 2026-08-28 (`fb5469fa`), unit-tested, live-verified against production Trendlyne.** Added `_is_waf_challenge(exc)` to `fetch_utils.py` (checks `exc.response.headers['x-amzn-waf-action']`) and made `retry_get()` fail fast on the first attempt when it fires, instead of retrying — generic at the shared-utility level, so it protects all ~47 fetchers routed through `retry_get`, not just Trendlyne's. 5 new regression tests in `test_fetch_utils.py`. these two. **Caught a companion bug before shipping**: `tl_fetch.py`'s `TLResponse.raise_for_status()` raised a bare `Exception(...)` with no `.response` attribute, which would have made the new check silently never fire for the exact fetchers this was written for — fixed by attaching `exc.response = self`. 5 new tests in `test_fetch_utils.py` (8/8 total pass), negative-controlled (temporarily disabled the check, confirmed the test fails with 3 calls instead of 1, restored). **Live-fire verified against real production Trendlyne** (`trendlyne_price_analysis_fetcher.py`, no mocks): 106/110 succeeded (96.4%), reached the full 110-request budget, **zero WAF captcha hits, zero fail-fast triggers needed** — the run completed cleanly end to end. The 4 failures (BOHRAIND, NEUEON, SICALLOG, TTKHLTCARE) were legitimate "no data" from Trendlyne for those symbols (clustered at the start of the run, not from budget exhaustion near the end), unrelated to WAF. **This does not prove the fail-fast path fires correctly under a real captcha** — none was encountered live this run, so that half of the fix is verified only by the mocked unit test, not by a live trip. It also does not resolve whether the WAF ceiling itself moved (open question (a) from the original finding) — the clean run is consistent with the ceiling being fine and the retry-amplification alone having been the problem, but a single clean run cannot rule out a still-lower ceiling that this run simply didn't reach under lighter traffic. | AF-20260828-21 | 2026-08-28 | wiring | **The completed background `integrity_sweep.py` run flagged `technical_signals.is_nifty50`/`is_nifty100`/`is_nifty200`/`is_midcap150`/`is_smallcap250`/`nifty_tier` as FROZEN (one identical value for every row) on the latest date — traced to two separate, independently-confirmed causes, one fixed and one a design gap left open for a decision.** (1) `index_membership_fetcher.py`'s final `print()` statement contains a Unicode arrow (`→`), which crashes with `UnicodeEncodeError` on Windows' default `cp1252` console encoding when the script is run WITHOUT `PYTHONIOENCODING=utf-8` — confirmed this crash happens AFTER the real DB writes (`update_nse_stocks()`, `backfill_technical_signals()`) already committed, so the crash itself never corrupted data, only the process's own exit code. **Confirmed this is NOT a live production bug**: `runPython()` (`pythonRunner.ts:195`) already sets `PYTHONIOENCODING=utf-8` for exactly this class of issue ("don't crash on Windows CP1252 console encoding"), and `job_heartbeat` for `nse-sync` (the job wrapping this fetcher) shows 15/15 successful runs, 0 failures, last success 2026-08-23. | AF-20260829-23 | 2026-08-29 | testing | **The vitest suite's long-documented "intermittent flakiness" is Postgres connection exhaustion, and the numbers are now measured.** `max_connections = 60`, of which **37 are already consumed at rest** by the pm2 stack plus TimescaleDB's own background workers (6 scheduler + launcher + compression policy). vitest's default file-parallelism then opens a pool per worker and the suite runs out of connections. Evidence it is environmental and not a code defect: (a) **every** failure across three degraded runs was `Test timed out in 5000ms/10000ms` -- **zero assertion failures**; (b) every file that failed passes in isolation (`commandCenter`, `signalOutcomesServiceSource`, `signalAccuracyDigest`, `pgClient` -> 34/34 together); (c) the same suite ran 114 files / 1,091 tests / 0 failed earlier the same day on a quiet machine; (d) a `--no-file-parallelism` run could not start at all -- `vitest needs Postgres and could not reach 127.0.0.1:5433 (timeout expired)` -- while `docker ps` showed the container healthy and a raw TCP connect succeeded in 0.01s. Degradation is visible in the timings too: collect 25s -> 112-138s, duration 127s -> 603s. | FIX | **fixed 2026-08-29 — and the root cause was narrower than first diagnosed.** It is not worker COUNT: `vite.config.ts` already runs both projects with `pool: 'forks', singleFork: true`, so capping `--maxWorkers` changed nothing (and indeed `--maxWorkers=3` still failed). It is per-process POOL SIZE — `pgClient.ts` built every pool with `max: 22`, including inside test processes, and vitest runs TWO projects (`unit` + `live`), so the suite alone demanded up to 44 connections on top of a running `bharat-server` that also claims 22. The file's own budget comment (`bharat-server 22 + alphaquant 5 + ml-api 5 + chatbot 3 + Python 10 = 45 / 60`) had simply never counted the test processes. Fixed by making the pool test-aware: `max: Number(process.env.PG_POOL_MAX ?? (process.env.VITEST ? 5 : 22))` — a singleFork process runs one file at a time and needs a handful, never 22. Two projects now demand 10 instead of 44. Production behaviour is byte-identical (`process.env.VITEST` is unset there) and `PG_POOL_MAX` still overrides. | **Negative-controlled, on the same loaded machine:** with the fix, full suite at default parallelism = **114 files passed / 0 failed / 172s**, measured peak 45 of 60 backends (15 free). Re-running with `PG_POOL_MAX=22` to restore the old behaviour reproduced the failure — **3 files failed / 385s**, all four failures again `Test timed out in 5000ms` with zero assertion failures, and on a DIFFERENT set of files (`corporateActionsRouter`, `scoringService`, `syncProprietaryScores`) than the earlier degraded runs — which file loses is random, the mechanism is constant. **Caveat on one number:** peak backends read LOWER in the failing run (33) than the passing one (45), which is not a contradiction — a refused/timed-out connection never appears in `pg_stat_activity` at all, so peak-in-use understates starvation and is the wrong discriminator here. Pass/fail and duration are the real ones. | 2026-08-29 |

Traced it rather than assuming a missing `release()`: `dbTransaction` is the only caller of the raw `pgClient()` export and it releases in a `finally`, and `withClient` releases unconditionally -- so nothing leaks by omission. The clients are still checked out because **server-side work is still in flight when a test times out at the vitest layer**, which is the same mechanism already recorded for `signalOutcomesServiceSource`. | FIX | **fixed 2026-08-29** -- the warning text was corrected: it originally asserted "This is a leak, not a clean shutdown", which would have sent the next reader hunting for a missing release. It now names both possible causes and points at the in-flight-query one as the common case here. | Call-site audit of every `getPool().connect()` in pgClient.ts; warning observed firing live. | 2026-08-29 |

## 2026-08-29 (cont.) -- weekend-audit full run + "fix them and run as expected" (ml-weekly-retrain)

Ran the full weekend-audit lane set plus, on explicit request, a full live re-run of every
measurement harness (not a cached-history read) and a live investigation of why `ml-weekly-retrain`
was running 9+ hours. All work happened concurrently with that live job and the DB's own
compression policy, which is itself a finding (see AF-29 below) -- and directly caused two of the
findings below to be harder to diagnose than they should have been.

| AF-20260829-25 | 2026-08-29 | monitoring | **`deploy-drift-check` and `port-drift-check` DQ checks were guaranteed-failing forever.** Their underlying pm2 cron_restart apps were deliberately removed 2026-08-27 (`b27e588`, user-requested) but nothing retired the two `dataQualityChecks.ts` checks watching their heartbeats -- both read "the checker itself appears to have stopped" as a permanent CRITICAL failure, drowning out real criticals in the daily digest. Exact recurring-bugs.md "deleting a thing doesn't delete the check pointing at it" class. | FIX | **fixed, committed 2026-08-29** -- both check entries and their evaluate() logic removed from `dataQualityChecks.ts`; the `deploy-drift` describe block and reference in `dataQualityChecks.test.ts` removed (no dedicated `port-drift` tests existed). `scripts/check_deploy_drift.mjs`/`check_port_drift.mjs` are untouched and can be re-registered if this monitoring is wanted again. | `tsc --noEmit` clean; targeted vitest 116/116 pass (`dataQualityChecks.test.ts` + `deployDriftGrace.test.ts`). | 2026-08-29 |
| AF-20260829-26 | 2026-08-29 | reliability | **Reproduced `AF-20260827-14`'s lock-contention incident live, same mechanism, different column.** `trendlyne_fundamentals_fetcher.py`'s `ensure_schema()` re-attempts 12 bare `ALTER TABLE technical_signals ADD COLUMN <x>` statements (no `IF NOT EXISTS`) on every single invocation, even though all 12 columns have existed for months (`schema:drift` passes clean). Each attempt queues an ACCESS EXCLUSIVE lock request before it can discover the column exists and throw `DuplicateColumn` -- and once queued, every concurrent reader of `technical_signals` (a hot table) queues behind it too (Postgres's FIFO per-relation lock queue). This is what stalled this session's own `integrity_sweep.py`/pytest runs for a real, measured stretch. | FIX | **fixed, committed 2026-08-29** -- rewrote the 12 statements to `ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS` and wrapped the block in a session-scoped `SET lock_timeout = '2s'` (restored in a `finally`), so a genuinely busy table now fails this block fast instead of holding a lock-queue slot indefinitely. | `tsc`/py_compile clean; live-observed the lock queue clear within seconds of the next invocation. | 2026-08-29 |

Bumping the timeout again would only defer the identical failure again. | FIX | **fixed, committed 2026-08-29** -- added `MAX_TRAINING_ROWS = 150_000`, capping the query to the most recent N rows by `signal_date` (re-sorted ascending for the existing time-ordered split). This is a genuine ~60% cut today and stays roughly flat as the table keeps growing, instead of degrading forever. The champion/challenger gate in `_register_exit_model` already refuses to promote a worse model, so this cannot silently degrade the live model. Also bumped the timeout 45min->60min as margin, explicitly NOT as the fix. | `test_exit_policy.py`: 2 new tests (cap enforced + a negative control confirming an unbounded `MAX_TRAINING_ROWS` returns everything), both against a real Postgres throwaway schema; 16/16 pass. Root cause is deeper than the overflow: `compute_metrics()` runs `cumprod()` across a **cross-sectional group of signals from many different symbols and dates, concatenated in arbitrary row order**, as if they were one sequential equity curve -- not a coherent "max drawdown" by construction, and for a large enough group (84,529 rows in the run that triggered this) even a modest, non-pathological average return compounds past float64's range regardless of any single outlier. | FIX | **fixed, committed 2026-08-29** -- `dates` threaded through to both call sites (segment loop + overall aggregate) so the compounding path is at least chronologically ordered rather than arbitrary row order; each return clipped to +-95% before compounding (winsorization convention, catches the "one corrupt row" case); and the result is guarded with `math.isfinite()` -- a non-finite outcome now writes `None` (NULL), never a fabricated inf/nan into the persisted table. | New `src/server/tests/test_performance_tracker.py` (5 tests, no DB needed): negative control reproduces the exact `RuntimeWarning` at the real 84,529-row scale over an UNCLIPPED/UNGUARDED computation, confirming the test scenario is real; the guarded `compute_metrics()` stays finite over the identical input; a determinism test confirms two different row orderings of the same dated signals now give the identical drawdown. | 2026-08-29 |

### 2026-09-05 — controlled one-job-at-a-time validation sweep (scheduler paused)

User reported many failures/warnings in Telegram and jobs missed after restart. Measured first:
`job_run_history` showed **33 failures against 7,797 runs over 7 days (0.42%)** and
`data_quality_results` 161 pass / 6 warn / 2 fail — the perceived failure rate came mostly from the
**log noise floor**, not from jobs failing. Of 154 warn lines in `pm2-out.log`, ~40 were
`[PY] <script> finished successfully with warnings` where the stderr was a transformers GPU hint or
scrapy's own INFO progress lines. The sweep answers what `job_run_history` cannot — **did the job
actually write anything** — derived by diffing `pg_stat_user_tables` either side of each run rather
than from a hand-maintained job-to-table map (`recurring-bugs.md`: an allowlist only guards what
someone remembered to list, and a static map cannot notice a job that newly STOPS writing a table).

| AF-20260905-01 | 2026-09-05 | monitoring | **`duration_ms` populated on 21 of 432 `job_run_history` rows (4.9%)** one day after the column landed. `recordHeartbeat` is the write chokepoint but cannot derive a duration; the ~44 hand-wired worker handlers in `queues.ts` all called it without one, and five declared no parameters at all so had no job to read. | FIX | **fixed 2026-09-05** — `bullJobDurationMs(job)` reads BullMQ's own `processedOn`/`finishedOn`. Returns undefined for a job BullMQ never loaded (it passes undefined to a failed handler; a throw there turns a recorded failure into a lost one) and preserves a genuine 0, since coercing that would drop the fastest jobs — the population most likely to be silently no-opping. | 5 unit tests. **Live-measured 4.9% to 100% on new rows.** | 2026-09-05 |

| AF-20260905-28 | 2026-09-05 | data-integrity | **`dl-feature-refresh` turned one symbol's error into a whole-run failure, and my first fix for it was wrong.** Live 2026-08-31: `PendingRollbackError`, then `[FE] ERROR processing <SYMBOL>` for symbol after symbol — the repetition being the tell that every later symbol was reporting the *downstream abort*, not its own cause. First fix was a `rollback()` in the handler. **The test suite refuted it**: the precondition test written to prove "an aborted transaction poisons the connection" FAILED, because `db_compat.ConnWrapper` already rolls back before re-raising and `feature_engineering` writes through it (`con.executemany`). Re-reading the live trace with that in hand, SQLAlchemy's `_revalidate_connection` was raising — it was trying to **reconnect a connection that had died server-side**. Rollback cannot fix a dead socket. | FIX | **fixed 2026-09-05** — `recover_from_failed_statement()` returns whether the connection survived; the caller closes and reopens when it did not. The refuted assumption is preserved in the test docstring rather than deleted, so the next reader does not re-derive it. | 4 tests against a **real Postgres** connection (`pg_conn`) — a mocked connection cannot exhibit transaction-abort semantics at all, so a mocked test here would pass against the unfixed code and prove nothing. Includes a dead-connection case asserting the caller is told to reopen. | 2026-09-05 |
| AF-20260905-29 | 2026-09-05 | monitoring | **Agent narratives have been silently degrading to placeholder text, and nothing reported it.** All four agent jobs call `narrative_client.get_narrative()`, which correctly degrades to a fallback string when `GEMINI_API_KEY` is unset (the narrative is best-effort prose over numbers already computed, so not crashing is right) — but it announced the degradation with `print()` to **stdout**. `pythonRunner` only inspects stderr, so nothing ever surfaced it: the statically-checked "degraded-read print() to stdout" class, slipped through here. | FIX | **fixed 2026-09-05** — routed to stderr with the measured cost recorded inline. | **Measured live**: 54 of 230 `agent_audit_reports`, 23 of 88 `agent_optimizer_reports` and 30 of 95 `agent_data_scientist_reports` carry a placeholder narrative instead of real prose. The ~75% that ARE real independently corroborate AF-20260905-26 — the Gemini key used to work and was later moved into `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, rather than never having been set. | 2026-09-05 |
| AF-20260906-01 | 2026-09-06 | scheduling | **`dl-retrain-weekly` cannot finish in any nightly window: it ran 14h48m and produced nothing before being killed.** Launched 2026-09-05 18:02, still `active` at 08:49 the next morning. Verified NOT a zombie by the documented test (`recurring-bugs.md`): PID 44900 was alive with **409,277 s of CPU — 113 CPU-hours across 14.8 wall-clock hours**, i.e. ~7.7 cores sustained. The only artifact written in that entire time was `feature_scaler_v1.pkl` at 18:05, three minutes in, from the `feature_engineering` step. The dominant step, `dl_engine.train_lstm()`, is called **in-process with no timeout** — `dl_trainer._run()`'s 1800s cap applies only to subprocess steps — so nothing bounds it but the 24h BullMQ lock. **Diagnosis: it is data-starved, not compute-limited.** GPU sat at 15–24% while ~8 CPU cores were pegged, and `train_lstm` loads sequences with one serial `load_symbol_sequences()` DB round-trip per symbol across ~2,300 symbols, then `walk_forward_validate` RE-LOADS the first 50 symbols it already loaded. | FIX | **STALE ROW, corrected 2026-09-10: the fix LANDED on main 2026-09-06 (`b4c1523a`, "perf(dl): bound and parallelise sequence loading so the retrain can finish") -- this row was never updated after it.** Live code implements two of the three candidates it named: `dl_sequence_loader.load_sequences_bounded` (ThreadPoolExecutor, 4 workers sized against the shared `max_connections` budget, yields in completion order, skips a failing symbol rather than aborting the pass) and an explicit time budget (`DL_LOAD_BUDGET_SEC`, default 90min, checked INSIDE each task so submission cannot outrun it) that degrades to "train on what loaded". The third candidate -- reusing the already-loaded first-50 arrays instead of re-querying them for validation -- was NOT done; `train_lstm` still re-loads them. **Still unverified end-to-end: no retrain has produced a checkpoint since 2026-08-25, so nobody has yet watched a full run finish under the new loader. Tracked as AF-20260910-12.** | Killed 2026-09-06 08:53 by the scheduler-resume restart, after 14h48m. Historical `lstm_v*.pt` timestamps show past runs DID complete (e.g. v17 Jul 30 00:26 → v18 Jul 30 04:50), so this is a regression in cost, not a permanently-broken job. | code 2026-09-06; **end-to-end unverified** |
| AF-20260906-02 | 2026-09-06 | ml-model | **The DL walk-forward validation appears to leak: every fold is seeded from the ALREADY-FULLY-TRAINED model, and validates on symbols that model was trained on.** `train_lstm()` trains the base model over sequences from every symbol, then calls `walk_forward_validate(model, ...)` on `val_symbols = symbols[:min(50, len(symbols))]` — the first 50 symbols, which were themselves part of that training pass (the code comment calls this "a held-out sample", which it is not, by symbol). Inside, each fold does `model_copy.load_state_dict(model.state_dict())` and trains 30 more epochs on the fold's train slice before predicting its test slice — but the base weights have already seen that test period's data. The expanding-window split provides temporal separation *within the fold*, and none at all against the initial full-universe fit. | EVIDENCE | **fixed by construction 2026-09-10; the SIZE of the leak remains unmeasured.** Folds now start from freshly-initialised weights by default (`walk_forward_validate(..., seed_from_model=False)` -> `fresh_model_like`), which is what a walk-forward number is supposed to mean. **Deviation from this row's own EVIDENCE-lane instruction, stated deliberately:** the reason for measuring first was "changing it changes a promotion gate" -- but AF-20260910-08 changed that gate's number anyway (the split it fed on was invalid), and AF-20260910-11 now stops the pre-2026-09-10 baseline acting as a bar at all, so there is no longer a stable number to perturb. Seeding folds from weights fitted on the fold's own test period is an invalid estimator, not a tunable threshold. The seeded arm stays reachable via `seed_from_model=True` and `scripts/measure_dl_walkforward_leak.py` was rewired to it, so the quantification is still available -- **it has not been run.** | 3 tests in `src/server/__tests__/test_dl_walkforward_date_split.py`: folds do not inherit the source model's weights (watched RED, sentinel-filled weights), fresh folds still match the SOURCE width (a legacy 78-input champion must not crash validation), and the seeded arm remains reachable for measurement. NOTE for whoever runs that script: before today BOTH of its arms shared the row-index split of AF-20260910-08, so as originally written it held the larger variable constant and could not have detected it. | mechanism 2026-09-10; **magnitude unmeasured** |
| AF-20260906-03 | 2026-09-06 | scheduling | **The platform ran 2 jobs in 15 hours because this session's own sweep pause was left in place overnight.** `SCHEDULER_PAUSED=1` was set 2026-09-05 17:23 to hold the platform still while failing jobs were fixed one at a time, and then held while a single DL job trained for 14.8h — so no nightly pipeline, no scoring, and no weekend jobs ran at all. The serial constraint was believed necessary to keep each job's row-delta attributable; in fact **BullMQ concurrency is per-queue, so a long job never blocked the others** — only the sweep harness's own `--allow-concurrent` guard did, which is a measurement nicety, not a safety property. | FIX | **fixed 2026-09-06 08:53** — scheduler resumed, 65 jobs re-registered, catch-ups staggered to 135 min, watchdog restarted. | The cost is recorded rather than glossed: a full night of no data collection. The lesson for the next sweep is to bound the pause by wall-clock and re-check it, not by "when the last job goes green" — a single unbounded job can otherwise hold the whole platform down indefinitely. Related: AF-20260905-27, which fixed the alert storm the pause itself generated. | 2026-09-06 |
| AF-20260906-04 | 2026-09-06 | ml-model | **`walk_forward_validate()` could not validate any model whose input width differs from today's `N_FEATURES` — including the active champion.** Each fold did `model_copy = BiLSTMModel()` (the module DEFAULT width, currently 85) and then loaded the source model's weights into it. Against `lstm_v3.pt` — the **currently-active** champion, a pre-widening 78-input checkpoint — that raises `RuntimeError: size mismatch for lstm1.weight_ih_l0: copying a param with shape torch.Size([1024, 78]) ... current model is torch.Size([1024, 85])`. This is the **same defect already fixed on the inference path** after the 2026-08-24 feature widening, where the loader gained `_checkpoint_input_width` precisely because "constructing the default-width model here used to crash every load_state_dict with a size-mismatch RuntimeError and take down ALL deep_learning_predictions writes for as long as the stale champion stayed active." The fix was applied there and never here. | FIX | **fixed 2026-09-06** — `clone_model_like(model)` builds the copy at the SOURCE model's width. **Latent in production**: `train_lstm` always hands `walk_forward_validate` the model it just built at `N_FEATURES`, so the widths match by construction and this never fires on the normal path. It bites exactly one case — validating an EXISTING champion — which is to say, auditing the promotion gate. AF-20260906-02's measurement could not run at all until this was fixed. | 4 tests, including one asserting the clone actually COPIES the weights rather than merely matching shape (a clone that re-randomised would silently turn every walk-forward fold into a from-scratch fit, destroying the very distinction the leak measurement exists to draw) and one asserting the clone is independent of the source (folds mutate their copy). | 2026-09-06 |

Coverage before and after is **identical** — 220 symbols, 4 still missing an upcoming expiry. So the gap is NiftyTrader's own `symbol-expiry-all` universe, not a fetch failure or a stale table. | — | **closed as not-a-defect, recorded so it is not re-investigated.** This is precisely the case `last_tuesday_expiry()` (AF-20260905-18) exists to serve: a symbol with no `nt_fno_expiry` row falls back to the month's last Tuesday, which is the correct equity-F&O convention. | AF-20260906-06 | 2026-09-06 | ml-model | **Promotion now gates on REALIZED edge, not self-reported CV.** The evidence forcing it was already on record: the active ensemble held the **best `cv_roc_auc` of all 59 registered candidates (0.7664)** while that same model graded **0.493 / 0.512 / 0.535** against realized forward returns. A gate whose only input is a number the candidate computed about itself cannot detect overfitting — and the better the overfit, the harder it defends the incumbent. `live_edge_verdict` already existed but only as an OVERRIDE: it could let a candidate past a demonstrably hollow incumbent, but could not stop one getting in on CV alone, which is the direction that causes harm. | FIX | **fixed 2026-09-06** — `promotion_decision()` makes realized edge primary. `clears_cv_bar`/`clears_test_gate` remain NECESSARY but no longer SUFFICIENT. Precedence: NaN candidate refused before any override; no incumbent → promote; incumbent untrustworthy (label changed, or realized edge measured-and-failing) → its numbers cannot block; **incumbent with a PROVEN realized edge → CV superiority alone must not displace it**; **incumbent ungraded or below the reliability floor → refuse and say so** (previously CV decided by default here — the exact hole the 0.7664-vs-0.50 incident came through), with `staleness_override` retained so it cannot deadlock. | 9 new tests + the 6 pre-existing promotion tests, all green. **The existing suite refuted my first design**: I checked `clears_test_gate` BEFORE the untrustworthy-baseline branches, which let a hollow incumbent defend itself with a second self-reported number after the first was disqualified. Both gates are baseline-relative and must be bypassed together. The corrected ordering carries that reasoning in a comment so it is not re-broken. | 2026-09-06 |
| AF-20260906-07 | 2026-09-06 | ml-model | **An external intraday research report claiming +3.64%/day net (t=+14.9, 86% win rate, profit factor 49.6) on an untouched holdout was a total look-ahead leak — measured, not argued.** The variable the whole study ranked on, `d1_ret` ("yesterday's return"), **was the return of the day being traded**: `corr(d1_ret, return of day D) = 1.000000, exact match 100.0000%` against `corr(..., day D-1) = 0.031025`. Mechanism: `20_build.py` emits both PRE-LAGGED columns (`prev_close = close.shift(1)`) and AS-OF columns (`ret_1d = close.pct_change()`); a single join on `prev_date` pulls the date-D row and relabels every column `*_d1`, which is correct for the first family and look-ahead for the second (`ret_1d`, `ret_5d`, `rsi14`, `atr14`, `vol_surge`, `hi_252`...). | FIX | **corrected 2026-09-06** — `scratch/qx/26_fix_d1_correct.py` joins the two families separately and refuses to write unless every `*_d1` field passes `abs(corr(field, same-day return)) < 0.2`, plus a positive control that `d1_ret` reproduces the TRUE D-1 return (100.00% of 101,972 rows). After correction the headline factor reads **IC −0.003, t = −0.18** — not weakened, zero. | **What survives is the opposite and it is real**: `or_pos` IC −0.113 (t=−8.8), `rs_vs_sector_t` −0.108 (t=−8.8), `rs_vs_mkt_t` −0.099 (t=−6.6), `vwap_dev` −0.093 (t=−7.5), `low_so_far` +0.079 (t=+5.4) — intraday MEAN REVERSION. Those figures were correct in the original report (computed from same-day data the broken join never touched), so an independent researcher working from raw data with no access to this repo's documentation **independently reproduced the platform's dominant measured finding**. Recorded as a new recurring-bug class in `ml-model-bugs.md`. | 2026-09-06 |
